I guess… what brought me in is I just don’t feel like myself. Like the warm part of me shut off or something. I… I don’t think so? Not most of the day, nearly every day. I get in moods, but it’s hard to separate what’s just life and work and being tired of everyone needing something.

I don’t know… maybe? I mean, I lose my place sometimes if I’m watching something, but half the time my husband is talking over it or the dog is scratching at the door, so I don’t know if that counts.

Yeah… that’s actually kind of… that’s the part that scared me, I guess.

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess… what brought me in is I just don’t feel like myself. Like the warm part of me shut off or something. I’ll be sitting with my husband, or trying to watch one of my shows after work—usually that kind of thing would make me feel settled, you know? Even a little happy. But lately there’s just… nothing there.\n\nAnd I know that sounds strange. I keep thinking maybe I’m just tired from work, because stocking shelves and filling orders, it wears you out, and people don’t realize how much you’re on your feet. But it’s more than that. I’m there, I know I should feel comfort, but I don’t. That’s why I came.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve stopped enjoying things that you used to find pleasurable, like spending time with your husband or watching your favorite shows?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that’s pretty much it. Like I’ll sit next to my husband and he’ll put on something we both usually laugh at, or I’ll try to watch one of my shows after work, and I know I should feel kind of comfortable, like “okay, I’m home now.” But it just doesn’t land.\n\nIt’s weird, because I still go through the motions. I sit there, I look at the TV, I’ll even have dinner or fold laundry—there’s always laundry, I swear—but inside it’s just kind of blank. Not even sad exactly. Just like the warm part of me turned off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it depressed exactly. I mean… I’ve had days where I’m low, sure, like anyone, especially after a long shift when the back room is a mess and everybody’s asking where things are like I personally hid them.\n\nBut most of the day, nearly every day? I’m not sure. It’s more like I’m just kind of… flat. Like I’m there, doing what I’m supposed to do, but I don’t really know what I’m feeling. Does that count as depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even after getting enough rest?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m tired after work, but I’m always tired after work, you know? Stocking and filling orders, you’re lifting things and walking back and forth all day, and sometimes the carts squeak so loud it gets in your head. They really need to fix those.\n\nBut even if I sleep okay… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of whether I feel rested or not. It’s hard to tell what’s normal tired and what isn’t. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big change in my energy, not clearly.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty about things lately, or like you’re not worth as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I mean… I don’t know. I feel bad about little things, like if I snap at my husband or if I forget something at work, but I’ve always done that. Everybody messes up sometimes.\n\nThe “not worth as much” part… I’m not sure I think that exactly. I still get up, go to work, do what I’m supposed to do. I mean, there was this one manager who used to make everyone feel useless if a shelf wasn’t filled right, and I hated that—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I haven’t noticed some big change where I’m walking around thinking I’m worthless or anything. It’s more just… I don’t know how to answer that cleanly.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you've been feeling down or in a depressed mood most of the day, nearly every day, not just during work or after a long shift?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think so? Not most of the day, nearly every day. I get in moods, but it’s hard to separate what’s just life and work and being tired of everyone needing something.\n\nLike even at home, sometimes I’m just quiet, and my husband asks if I’m mad, and I’m not mad—I just don’t have much to say. But then I’ll be doing dishes or folding towels, and I’ll think about how we need to replace the little mat by the sink because the corner keeps curling up and I trip on it every single time… sorry.\n\nSo, no, I can’t really say I’m depressed most of the day nearly every day. I’m not sure that’s what it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's been harder to focus on things lately, like following along with a show or making decisions at work or home?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? I mean, I lose my place sometimes if I’m watching something, but half the time my husband is talking over it or the dog is scratching at the door, so I don’t know if that counts.\n\nAt work there’s always a lot going on—labels, carts, people asking where things are—and I’ve always had to kind of repeat things in my head. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some clear thing where I suddenly can’t focus, though. Decisions… I don’t know, I’m not great with decisions anyway, like even picking what to make for dinner turns into checking the freezer and then realizing we’re out of trash bags.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. Nothing really stands out to me as different, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble thinking clearly or making decisions lately, even when you're not distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, when is anything ever not distracting, you know? Even if the house is quiet, I’ll start thinking about laundry or whether I paid the electric bill, and then I’m up checking something.\n\nBut thinking clearly… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big problem. I still decide things when I have to. Sometimes I stand there too long, like with dinner or what errand to do first, but I’ve always kind of done that. They really need to fix those.\n\nBut even if I sleep okay… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of whether I feel rested or not. It’s hard to tell what’s normal tired and what isn’t. I mean, there was this one manager who used to make everyone feel useless if a shelf wasn’t filled right, and I hated that—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I haven’t noticed some big change where I’m walking around thinking I’m worthless or anything. It’s more just… I don’t know how to answer that cleanly.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you've been feeling down or in a depressed mood most of the day, nearly every day, not just during work or after a long shift?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think so? Does that count as depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even after getting enough rest?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m tired after work, but I’m always tired after work, you know? Stocking and filling orders, you’re lifting things and walking back and forth all day, and sometimes the carts squeak so loud it gets in your head. They really need to fix those.\n\nBut even if I sleep okay… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of whether I feel rested or not. It’s hard to tell what’s normal tired and what isn’t. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big change in my energy, not clearly.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty about things lately, or like you’re not worth as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I mean… I don’t know. I feel bad about little things, like if I snap at my husband or if I forget something at work, but I’ve always done that. Everybody messes up sometimes.\n\nThe “not worth as much” part… I’m not sure I think that exactly. I still get up, go to work, do what I’m supposed to do. I mean, there was this one manager who used to make everyone feel useless if a shelf wasn’t filled right, and I hated that—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I haven’t noticed some big change where I’m walking around thinking I’m worthless or anything. It’s more just… I don’t know how to answer that cleanly.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you've been feeling down or in a depressed mood most of the day, nearly every day, not just during work or after a long shift?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think so? Not most of the day, nearly every day. I get in moods, but it’s hard to separate what’s just life and work and being tired of everyone needing something.\n\nLike even at home, sometimes I’m just quiet, and my husband asks if I’m mad, and I’m not mad—I just don’t have much to say. But then I’ll be doing dishes or folding towels, and I’ll think about how we need to replace the little mat by the sink because the corner keeps curling up and I trip on it every single time… sorry.\n\nSo, no, I can’t really say I’m depressed most of the day nearly every day. I’m not sure that’s what it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's been harder to focus on things lately, like following along with a show or making decisions at work or home?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? I mean, I lose my place sometimes if I’m watching something, but half the time my husband is talking over it or the dog is scratching at the door, so I don’t know if that counts.\n\nAt work there’s always a lot going on—labels, carts, people asking where things are—and I’ve always had to kind of repeat things in my head. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some clear thing where I suddenly can’t focus, though. Decisions… I don’t know, I’m not great with decisions anyway, like even picking what to make for dinner turns into checking the freezer and then realizing we’re out of trash bags.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. Nothing really stands out to me as different, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble thinking clearly or making decisions lately, even when you're not distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, when is anything ever not distracting, you know? Even if the house is quiet, I’ll start thinking about laundry or whether I paid the electric bill, and then I’m up checking something.\n\nBut thinking clearly… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big problem. I still decide things when I have to. Sometimes I stand there too long, like with dinner or what errand to do first, but I’ve always kind of done that. My mom used to joke I could spend ten minutes picking cereal, which is probably not what you mean.\n\nSo… no, not really, or at least I can’t tell that it’s different lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t enjoy things you used to, even small things outside of work or chores—like a favorite snack, a walk, or talking to a friend?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s actually kind of… that’s the part that scared me, I guess.\n\nIt’s not even like, “Oh, I don’t like my hobbies anymore,” because I don’t have some big hobby. I mean, after work I usually just want to sit down, maybe watch one of my shows with my husband, and normally that feels nice, like just being next to him. Or I’ll have something little I like, and it should feel comforting.\n\nBut lately it’s like the warm part of me just… shut off. I’ll be sitting there and I know I should feel happy, or calm, or at least glad to be home, and there’s just nothing. Like I’m there, but I’m not really getting anything from it. That’s honestly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've been feeling this way—like the warm part of you is shut off—for most days over the last two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I mean, I’m trying to think if there was a day where I felt normal, and maybe there were little moments, like if the dog does something stupid or my husband makes a joke, but it doesn’t really land the same.\n\nLike, I’ll sit with him and put on one of my shows, and normally that would feel comforting—just being home, you know? But lately it’s like that warm part of me is shut off. I know I should feel happy or settled or something, and there’s just… nothing there. That’s honestly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve completely lost interest in things you used to enjoy, like watching your favorite shows or spending time with your husband?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “completely” lost interest, exactly. Like, I still turn the show on, and I still sit with my husband, and I want it to feel normal. It’s just… it doesn’t do anything inside me.\n\nHonestly, that’s what made me come in. I’ll be sitting there after work, trying to watch something I usually like, or just being next to him, and I know I should feel comforted or a little happy, and there’s just nothing there. Like that warm part of me shut off.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you're feeling much less pleasure than usual from things you used to enjoy, like watching your favorite shows or spending time with your husband?\n\nPatient: Yeah… much less. I mean, I still do the things, like I’ll put the show on or sit next to my husband on the couch, but it’s like I’m going through the motion of it.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds small, but that’s kind of what made me come in. Because normally those little things are what get me through the day, you know? Like after work, even if I’m tired and my feet hurt, I can sit down and feel like, okay, I’m home. But now I sit there and I’m just… blank. Like the warm part of me shut off or something. I know I should feel comforted, or even a little happy, and there’s just nothing there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean… I’ve had days where I’m low, sure, like anyone, especially after a long shift when the back room is a mess and everybody’s asking where things are like I personally hid them.\n\nBut most of the day, nearly every day? I’m not sure. It’s more like I’m just kind of… flat. Like I’m there, doing what I’m supposed to do, but I don’t really know what I’m feeling. Does that count as depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even after getting enough rest?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m tired after work, but I’m always tired after work, you know? Stocking and filling orders, you’re lifting things and walking back and forth all day, and sometimes the carts squeak so loud it gets in your head. They really need to fix those.\n\nBut even if I sleep okay… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of whether I feel rested or not. It’s hard to tell what’s normal tired and what isn’t. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big change in my energy, not clearly.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty about things lately, or like you’re not worth as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I mean… I don’t know. I feel bad about little things, like if I snap at my husband or if I forget something at work, but I’ve always done that. Everybody messes up sometimes.\n\nThe “not worth as much” part… I’m not sure I think that exactly. I still get up, go to work, do what I’m supposed to do. I mean, there was this one manager who used to make everyone feel useless if a shelf wasn’t filled right, and I hated that—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I haven’t noticed some big change where I’m walking around thinking I’m worthless or anything. It’s more just… I don’t know how to answer that cleanly.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you've been feeling down or in a depressed mood most of the day, nearly every day, not just during work or after a long shift?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think so? Not most of the day, nearly every day. I get in moods, but it’s hard to separate what’s just life and work and being tired of everyone needing something.\n\nLike even at home, sometimes I’m just quiet, and my husband asks if I’m mad, and I’m not mad—I just don’t have much to say. But then I’ll be doing dishes or folding towels, and I’ll think about how we need to replace the little mat by the sink because the corner keeps curling up and I trip on it every single time… sorry.\n\nSo, no, I can’t really say I’m depressed most of the day nearly every day. I’m not sure that’s what it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's been harder to focus on things lately, like following along with a show or making decisions at work or home?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? I mean, I lose my place sometimes if I’m watching something, but half the time my husband is talking over it or the dog is scratching at the door, so I don’t know if that counts.\n\nAt work there’s always a lot going on—labels, carts, people asking where things are—and I’ve always had to kind of repeat things in my head. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some clear thing where I suddenly can’t focus, though. Decisions… I don’t know, I’m not great with decisions anyway, like even picking what to make for dinner turns into checking the freezer and then realizing we’re out of trash bags.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. Nothing really stands out to me as different, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble thinking clearly or making decisions lately, even when you're not distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, when is anything ever not distracting, you know? Even if the house is quiet, I’ll start thinking about laundry or whether I paid the electric bill, and then I’m up checking something.\n\nBut thinking clearly… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big problem. I still decide things when I have to. Sometimes I stand there too long, like with dinner or what errand to do first, but I’ve always kind of done that. My mom used to joke I could spend ten minutes picking cereal, which is probably not what you mean.\n\nSo… no, not really, or at least I can’t tell that it’s different lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t enjoy things you used to, even small things outside of work or chores—like a favorite snack, a walk, or talking to a friend?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s actually kind of… that’s the part that scared me, I guess.\n\nIt’s not even like, “Oh, I don’t like my hobbies anymore,” because I don’t have some big hobby. I mean, after work I usually just want to sit down, maybe watch one of my shows with my husband, and normally that feels nice, like just being next to him. Or I’ll have something little I like, and it should feel comforting.\n\nBut lately it’s like the warm part of me just… shut off. I’ll be sitting there and I know I should feel happy, or calm, or at least glad to be home, and there’s just nothing. Like I’m there, but I’m not really getting anything from it. That’s honestly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've been feeling this way—like the warm part of you is shut off—for most days over the last two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I mean, I’m trying to think if there was a day where I felt normal, and maybe there were little moments, like if the dog does something stupid or my husband makes a joke, but it doesn’t really land the same.\n\nLike, I’ll sit with him and put on one of my shows, and normally that would feel comforting—just being home, you know? But lately it’s like that warm part of me is shut off. I know I should feel happy or settled or something, and there’s just… nothing there. That’s honestly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve completely lost interest in things you used to enjoy, like watching your favorite shows or spending time with your husband?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “completely” lost interest, exactly. I’ll be sitting there after work, trying to watch something I usually like, or just being next to him, and I know I should feel comforted or a little happy, and there’s just nothing there. Like that warm part of me shut off.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you're feeling much less pleasure than usual from things you used to enjoy, like watching your favorite shows or spending time with your husband?\n\nPatient: Yeah… much less. I mean, I still do the things, like I’ll put the show on or sit next to my husband on the couch, but it’s like I’m going through the motion of it.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds small, but that’s kind of what made me come in. Because normally those little things are what get me through the day, you know? Like after work, even if I’m tired and my feet hurt, I can sit down and feel like, okay, I’m home. But now I sit there and I’m just… blank. Like the warm part of me shut off or something. I know I should feel comforted, or even a little happy, and there’s just nothing there.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer care about doing things you used to enjoy, even trying to make yourself do them doesn’t feel worth it?\n\nPatient: It’s not that I don’t care at all, I guess… I still try. Like I’ll put my show on after work, or sit with my husband because that’s what we usually do, and I keep thinking maybe if I just do the normal thing, I’ll feel normal.\n\nBut honestly, yeah, it feels kind of pointless sometimes because nothing really comes back from it. Like the warm part of me just shut off. I know I should feel comforted sitting there, or even a little happy, and instead it’s just… nothing. And then I start thinking about the laundry or what’s in the freezer for dinner, just to have something else in my head, but the whole time I’m sitting there like, why doesn’t this feel like anything?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve lost interest in most activities, even ones you used to enjoy, like watching your favorite shows or spending time with your husband?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say every single activity, because honestly lately I mostly just go to work, come home, figure out food, and then the day’s kind of gone. I was just telling my husband the other night that we keep buying those frozen dinners and then neither of us wants the same one, which is stupid, but—\n\nSorry. I mean, I still do the things, like I’ll put the show on or sit next to my husband on the couch, but it’s like I’m going through the motion of it.\n\nAnd I know that probably sounds small, but that’s kind of what made me come in. Because normally those little things are what get me through the day, you know? Like after work, even if I’m tired and my feet hurt, I can sit down and feel like, okay, I’m home. But now I sit there and I’m just… blank. Like the warm part of me shut off or something. I know I should feel comforted, or even a little happy, and there’s just nothing there.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer care about doing things you used to enjoy, even trying to make yourself do them doesn’t feel worth it?\n\nPatient: It’s not that I don’t care at all, I guess… I still try. Like I’ll put my show on after work, or sit with my husband because that’s what we usually do, and I keep thinking maybe if I just do the normal thing, I’ll feel normal.\n\nBut honestly, yeah, it feels kind of pointless sometimes because nothing really comes back from it. Like the warm part of me just shut off. I know I should feel comforted sitting there, or even a little happy, and instead it’s just… nothing. And then I start thinking about the laundry or what’s in the freezer for dinner, just to have something else in my head, but the whole time I’m sitting there like, why doesn’t this feel like anything?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve lost interest in most activities, even ones you used to enjoy, like watching your favorite shows or spending time with your husband?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say every single activity, because honestly lately I mostly just go to work, come home, figure out food, and then the day’s kind of gone. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I know I’ve kind of said it already, but that’s really the thing. I’ll come home and put on one of my shows, or I’ll sit next to my husband, and normally that would feel like… home, I guess.

But now it’s just flat. Like I’m there, and the TV is on, and he’s there, and I know I should feel comforted or even a little happy, but there’s nothing. And then I’ll start thinking about something dumb, like whether we need more paper towels or what’s still in the freezer, just because it’s easier than sitting there noticing that I don’t feel anything.

Honestly, that’s what scared me enough to come in. It feels like the warm part of me shut off.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3, F32.9, F33.0, F33.1, F33.2, F33.3, F33.9, F34.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess… what brought me in is I just don’t feel like myself. Like the warm part of me shut off or something. I’ll be sitting with my husband, or trying to watch one of my shows after work—usually that kind of thing would make me feel settled, you know? Even a little happy. But lately there’s just… nothing there.\n\nAnd I know that sounds strange. I keep thinking maybe I’m just tired from work, because stocking shelves and filling orders, it wears you out, and people don’t realize how much you’re on your feet. But it’s more than that. I’m there, I know I should feel comfort, but I don’t. That’s why I came.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve stopped enjoying things that you used to find pleasurable, like spending time with your husband or watching your favorite shows?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that’s pretty much it. Like I’ll sit next to my husband and he’ll put on something we both usually laugh at, or I’ll try to watch one of my shows after work, and I know I should feel kind of comfortable, like “okay, I’m home now.” But it just doesn’t land.\n\nIt’s weird, because I still go through the motions. I sit there, I look at the TV, I’ll even have dinner or fold laundry—there’s always laundry, I swear—but inside it’s just kind of blank. Not even sad exactly. Just like the warm part of me turned off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it depressed exactly. I mean… I’ve had days where I’m low, sure, like anyone, especially after a long shift when the back room is a mess and everybody’s asking where things are like I personally hid them.\n\nBut most of the day, nearly every day? I’m not sure. It’s more like I’m just kind of… flat. Like I’m there, doing what I’m supposed to do, but I don’t really know what I’m feeling. Does that count as depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even after getting enough rest?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m tired after work, but I’m always tired after work, you know? Stocking and filling orders, you’re lifting things and walking back and forth all day, and sometimes the carts squeak so loud it gets in your head. They really need to fix those.\n\nBut even if I sleep okay… I don’t know. I haven’t really been keeping track of whether I feel rested or not. It’s hard to tell what’s normal tired and what isn’t. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big change in my energy, not clearly.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty about things lately, or like you’re not worth as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I mean… I don’t know. I feel bad about little things, like if I snap at my husband or if I forget something at work, but I’ve always done that. Everybody messes up sometimes.\n\nThe “not worth as much” part… I’m not sure I think that exactly. I still get up, go to work, do what I’m supposed to do. I mean, there was this one manager who used to make everyone feel useless if a shelf wasn’t filled right, and I hated that—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I haven’t noticed some big change where I’m walking around thinking I’m worthless or anything. It’s more just… I don’t know how to answer that cleanly.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you've been feeling down or in a depressed mood most of the day, nearly every day, not just during work or after a long shift?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think so? Not most of the day, nearly every day. I get in moods, but it’s hard to separate what’s just life and work and being tired of everyone needing something.\n\nLike even at home, sometimes I’m just quiet, and my husband asks if I’m mad, and I’m not mad—I just don’t have much to say. But then I’ll be doing dishes or folding towels, and I’ll think about how we need to replace the little mat by the sink because the corner keeps curling up and I trip on it every single time… sorry.\n\nSo, no, I can’t really say I’m depressed most of the day nearly every day. I’m not sure that’s what it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's been harder to focus on things lately, like following along with a show or making decisions at work or home?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? I mean, I lose my place sometimes if I’m watching something, but half the time my husband is talking over it or the dog is scratching at the door, so I don’t know if that counts.\n\nAt work there’s always a lot going on—labels, carts, people asking where things are—and I’ve always had to kind of repeat things in my head. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some clear thing where I suddenly can’t focus, though. Decisions… I don’t know, I’m not great with decisions anyway, like even picking what to make for dinner turns into checking the freezer and then realizing we’re out of trash bags.\n\nSo… I’m not sure. Nothing really stands out to me as different, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble thinking clearly or making decisions lately, even when you're not distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, when is anything ever not distracting, you know? Even if the house is quiet, I’ll start thinking about laundry or whether I paid the electric bill, and then I’m up checking something.\n\nBut thinking clearly… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some big problem. I still decide things when I have to. Sometimes I stand there too long, like with dinner or what errand to do first, but I’ve always kind of done that. My mom used to joke I could spend ten minutes picking cereal, which is probably not what you mean.\n\nSo… no, not really, or at least I can’t tell that it’s different lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t enjoy things you used to, even small things outside of work or chores—like a favorite snack, a walk, or talking to a friend?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s actually kind of… that’s the part that scared me, I guess.\n\nIt’s not even like, “Oh, I don’t like my hobbies anymore,” because I don’t have some big hobby. I mean, after work I usually just want to sit down, maybe watch one of my shows with my husband, and normally that feels nice, like just being next to him. Or I’ll have something little I like, and it should feel comforting.\n\nBut lately it’s like the warm part of me just… shut off. I’ll be sitting there and I know I should feel happy, or calm, or at least glad to be home, and there’s just nothing. Like I’m there, but I’m not really getting anything from it. That’s honestly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've been feeling this way—like the warm part of you is shut off—for most days over the last two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I mean, I’m trying to think if there was a day where I felt normal, and maybe there were little moments, like if the dog does something stupid or my husband makes a joke, but it doesn’t really land the same.\n\nLike, I’ll sit with him and put on one of my shows, and normally that would feel comforting—just being home, you know? But lately it’s like that warm part of me is shut off. I know I should feel happy or settled or something, and there’s just… nothing there. That’s honestly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve completely lost interest in things you used to enjoy, like watching your favorite shows or spending time with your husband?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “completely” lost interest, exactly. Like, I still turn the show on, and I still sit with my husband, and I want it to feel normal. It’s just… it doesn’t do anything inside me.\n\nHonestly, that’s what made me come in. I’ll be sitting there after work, trying to watch something I usually like, or just being next to him, and I know I should feel comforted or a little happy, and there’s just nothing there. Like that warm part of me shut off.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you're feeling much less pleasure than usual from things you used to enjoy, like watching your favorite shows or spending time with your husband?\n\nPatient: Yeah… much less. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if “completely” is the right word, because I still do the things, you know? Like I still sit down after work and put on my shows, or I sit with my husband, and part of me keeps thinking if I just keep the routine going then maybe it’ll click back in.

But… yeah, the pleasure part is just not there. That’s honestly what made me come in. It feels like the warm part of me shut off. I know I should feel comforted being with him, or even just a little happy watching something I normally like, but there’s just nothing there.

And then I end up thinking about random stuff, like what we’re having for dinner or whether I forgot something at the store, because it’s easier than paying attention to how empty it feels.
